## The Jane Street lens — what's actually wrong

Beyond everything already documented, the deep read reveals systemic issues at the type system level:

**1. The `graph: Any` gap — single biggest problem**

The central domain object (the graph) has no type anywhere. It flows through the codebase as `Any`, causing a cascade of `getattr(graph, "run_id", "")` calls everywhere. This means the type checker provides zero guarantees over the graph's structure. One `Protocol`:
```python
class GraphLike(Protocol):
    run_id: str
    nodes: Mapping[str, NodeLike]
```
...would eliminate dozens of untyped dynamic dispatches and allow the checker to catch field name typos.

**2. `dict[str, Any]` on domain objects is a type-system lie**

Fields like `input_json: dict[str, Any]`, `axes: dict[str, Any]`, `data: dict[str, Any]` on dataclasses and DTOs have known shapes. The `Any` propagates downstream — every caller is forced into `Any`-land. These should be typed dicts, Pydantic models, or at minimum `TypeAlias` narrowing.

**3. Import-time filesystem side effects in Fermi files**

```python
SYSTEM_PROMPT = _load_system_prompt()  # executes at import time
```
This runs a filesystem read every time the module is imported. Tests that import this module hit the filesystem silently. An import failure crashes the process. Should be `@lru_cache` lazy-loaded inside `build_user_prompt`, or injected from the shell layer.

**4. `_ensure_database()` called inside read functions**

Every read operation (`load_question_qc`, `list_question_scorecards`) calls `_ensure_database()` which runs `create_all()` — a schema write — as a side effect. A read should never trigger DDL. Schema init belongs at startup, not inside every storage query.

**5. `assert` as a runtime guard**

`assert stored is not None` in production code is disabled with `-O`. This is not a guard, it's documentation that will silently vanish in optimised builds. Should be `raise RuntimeError(...)` or a typed result.

**6. `except Exception` with silent swallow**

In `conditional_edge_quality.py`, a bare `except Exception` converts a real error into a dict entry. The comment says `# pragma: no cover — defensive guard` — an untested exception branch that swallows errors is a silent failure path, not a guard.

**7. Private symbol imports across modules**

```python
from packages.storage.graph_store import _edges_payload, _write_json
```
Importing `_`-prefixed symbols from another module couples to internal implementation. These should either be made public or extracted to shared utilities.

**8. Truthiness bug in Fermi files**

```python
horizon_text = f"{horizon_days} days" if horizon_days else (horizon_bucket or "unknown")
```
If `horizon_days = 0`, this branch goes to `horizon_bucket` and produces `"unknown"` instead of `"0 days"`. Should be `if horizon_days is not None`.

**9. `str` where `Literal`/`StrEnum` belongs — throughout contracts**

`target_type: str  # "binary" | "categorical" | ...` with a comment is not a type. `classification: str = "binary"`, `relevance: str = "direct"` — all of these should be `StrEnum` or `Literal`. The comment-as-type pattern means every consumer is on the honour system.

**10. `""` as a `None` sentinel**

`model_used: str = ""` — empty string to mean "not set" is worse than `str | None = None`. An empty string is a valid string; you can't distinguish "not set" from "set to empty". Every downstream check has to do `if model_used:` instead of `if model_used is not None:`, which conflates absence with emptiness.

**11. Redundant `as_dict()` methods on frozen dataclasses**

Several dataclasses define `as_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]` manually. `dataclasses.asdict(instance)` does this for free and reflects field additions automatically. The manual methods go stale silently.

**12. `str(value or "").strip()` on already-`str`-typed parameters**

Confirmed at the line level: function signatures say `run_id: str` and the body does `str(run_id or "").strip()`. The type says it's a `str`, the code treats it as `str | None | Any`. One of those is lying. Fix the boundary, not the interior.

Adds noise, no safety benefit.\n3. `os.environ` **deep in domain code** — env vars read inside domain functions instead of at config/CLI edges.\n4. **Module-level model resolution constants** — `MY_MODEL = resolve_task_model(...)` at import time.\n5. **Inline system prompts** — hardcoded prompt strings instead of loading from `prompts/` via the prompt store.\n6. **Global mutation lazy imports** — `global _x` with `Any | None` sentinels instead of function-scoped imports.\n\n## Known instances (from <issue id=\"15c6f655-f34e-4a27-8fff-d068f32f5913\">SPI-228</issue> review)\n\n* `extract_causal_relationships()` in `indicator_extraction.py` — dead, never called (<issue id=\"f7cb8aa6-b26a-44c0-857c-aa627b2d4c73\">SPI-250</issue>)\n* `os.environ` in `_build_full_message_list()` in `indicator_extraction.py` (<issue id=\"f7cb8aa6-b26a-44c0-857c-aa627b2d4c73\">SPI-250</issue>)\n* Circular import lazy globals in `driver_indicator_literature.py` (<issue id=\"7ef805df-0d80-43d1-9edf-68379eddcfcc\">SPI-249</issue>)\n\n## Codebase scan findings (2026-05-12)\n\n### Defensive wrapping — 286 hits across non-test code\n\n`str(x or \"\")` pattern concentrated in storage mappers:\n\n- `packages/domain/qc/gating_store.py` — 14 instances\n- `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py` — 12 instances\n- `core/research_driver/data_model.py` — 12 instances\n- `packages/domain/qc/store.py` — 11 instances\n\nConfirmed at line level: function signatures declare `run_id: str` and the body does `str(run_id or \"\").strip()`. The type says it's a `str`, the code treats it as `str | None | Any`. One of them is lying — fix the boundary, not the interior.\n\n`dict(x or {})` wrapping: ~45 hits across 20 files. Identity list comprehensions (`[item for item in list(...)]`): 4 files.\n\n### `os.environ` in domain code — 16 files\n\nOutside anything named config/settings/cli: `llm_scorer.py`, `relationship_judgment.py`, `fermi_llm.py`, `conditional_edge_quality.py`, `question_summary_pipeline.py`, and others.\n\n### Global mutation sentinels — 18 instances\n\nMostly `packages/inference/llm/runtime.py` (likely intentional lazy loading) but also `llm_scorer.py`, `fermi_llm.py`, `llm_prior.py`.\n\n### Module-level `resolve_task_model` — 2 remaining\n\n- `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/web_search_client.py:37`\n- `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:44`\n\n### Inline system prompts — 2 instances\n\n- `research/question_domains.py:132`\n- `core/pipelines/question_set_evaluation.py:257`\n\nNote: `core/research_driver/conditional_edge_quality.py` also hardcodes its system prompt inline while the sibling `FermiConditionalEdgeQualityTool` in the same file correctly loads from the prompt store. Inconsistency within one file.\n\n### Zero-coverage files with real logic (from pytest --cov)\n\nOverall codebase coverage: 78%. Files at 0% that are not CLI entry points or external HTTP clients:\n\n| File | Stmts | Notes |\n|------|-------|-------|\n| `core/pipelines/question_set_evaluation.py` | 497 | Large pipeline, may be superseded |\n| `packages/domain/qc/research_defensibility_store.py` | 117 | Domain storage |\n| `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/web_search_client.py` | 138 | May be superseded |\n| `packages/domain/qc/track_record_loader.py` | 73 | Track record feature |\n| `packages/domain/qc/track_record_aware_judge.py` | 47 | Track record feature |\n| `fs10_validation/components/fs3_extraction.py` | 97 | Validation component |\n| `services/worker/run_config_context.py` | 17 | Nothing imports this |\n\nThe `track_record_loader` / `track_record_aware_judge` pair at 0% coverage with no callers looks like an incomplete or abandoned feature.\n\n### Backward-compat shims accumulating — 17 instances\n\nExplicit \"backward compat\" / \"will be removed\" comments in production code:\n\n- `packages/storage/database.py` lines 670, 2530, 2640 — two marked \"will be removed\"\n- `packages/storage/graph_store.py` lines 334, 380\n- `core/pipelines/graph_persistence.py:602`\n- `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:587`\n- `core/research_driver/question_workflow.py:23`\n- `core/research_driver/driver_ontology.py:316`\n- `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py:641`\n- `packages/domain/baselines/apply.py:268`\n\n### AI-boilerplate module docstrings — 103 files\n\nFiles with the `Role: / Where It Is Used: / Relies On: / Key Symbols:` docstring format. This is a recognisable AI generation pattern that stands out immediately to any experienced reviewer. Should be replaced with a single concise sentence stating what the module does.\n\n### Monster functions\n\n| Function | File | Size |\n|----------|------|------|\n| `_run_single_step_impl()` | `services/worker/research_driver_step_pipeline.py:404` | 1,416 lines |\n| `run_research_driver_for_question()` | `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py:1861` | 1,205 lines |\n| `factor_extraction.run()` | `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/insight_agent/factor_extraction.py:731` | 533 lines |\n\n`run_research_driver_over_questions()` in `research_driver_batch_pipeline.py:396` takes **45 parameters**. This is a config object that was never extracted.\n\n### God files\n\n| File | Lines |\n|------|-------|\n| `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/retrieval_packs.py` | 3,380 |\n| `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py` | 3,066 |\n| `packages/storage/database.py` | 2,643 |\n| `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py` | 2,015 |\n| `services/worker/research_driver_step_pipeline.py` | 1,957 |\n\n`retrieval_packs.py` also has 33 local imports — it touches almost every layer of the stack.\n\n### Duplication\n\n- `assess_candidate()`, `build_query()`, `search_candidates()`, `validate_scope_details()`, `iter_sources()` each appear 4–7 times inside `retrieval_packs.py` alone — copy-pasted classes rather than a proper class hierarchy\n- `build_user_prompt()` in 4 separate Fermi files (`fermi_target.py`, `fermi_driver.py`, `fermi_indicator.py`, `fermi_edge.py`) — the three `packages/domain/baselines/fermi_*.py` files are structurally identical (same `_load_system_prompt()`, same `FermiXxxBaseline(BaseModel)` shape, same `build_user_prompt()` signature) and should share a base class\n\nNote: `build_messages()` and `postprocess()` appearing across many tool files are `BaseLLMTool` protocol implementations — correct polymorphism, not duplication.\n\n### Files that should be split\n\n**`core/research/historical_data.py` (834 lines)** — three fully independent data fetchers with no shared state: `fetch_numeric_series`, `fetch_event_history`, `fetch_categorical_archive`. Each has its own helper cluster. Should become `numeric_series.py`, `event_history.py`, `categorical_archive.py`.\n\n**`services/api/mappers.py` (755 lines, 27 functions)** — three distinct mapping concerns: question/question-set mappers, graph/artifact mappers, worker job mappers. Should become `question_mappers.py`, `graph_mappers.py`, `job_mappers.py`.\n\n**`core/run_config.py` (683 lines, 31 functions)** — two separate config domains (\"run research\" and \"generate questions\") plus shared TOML loading helpers. Should become `run_research_config.py` and `generate_questions_config.py`.\n\n**`packages/inference/llm/tiers.py` (610 lines, 32 functions)** — three concerns: type definitions, config file loading, and resolution functions (`resolve_task_model` etc). The resolution functions are what everything imports; they're buried alongside raw TOML parsing.\n\n**`packages/storage/sql_repositories.py` (2,015 lines)** — question mappers/repository and graph/node/edge mappers/repository are two separate things. Should become `question_repository.py` and `graph_repository.py`.\n\n### `__init__.py` barrel files\n\n`packages/storage/__init__.py` — **455 lines.** Re-exports everything from every storage submodule. Makes the `sql_repositories.py` split harder, increases circular import risk, inflates the import graph for anything touching storage. Should be thinned to only re-export the stable public API surface. Fix alongside the `sql_repositories.py` split.\n\nOther large `__init__.py` files (`domain/qc` at 131, `contracts` at 91, `inference/llm` at 87) are legitimate public API surfaces — fine as-is.\n\n### `__all__` in non-`__init__` files\n\n`fs10_validation/calibration_sweep.py` exports ~41 names — symptom of the file doing too many jobs. `retrieval_packs.py` exports ~24 — consistent with god file. These are downstream of the god-file problem, not a separate fix.\n\n### Enums\n\nNot a problem. Only one enum with 10+ values: `ArtifactKind` (13 values) in `packages/contracts/artifacts.py`. Clean.\n\n---\n\n## Type system issues (deep review findings, 2026-05-12)\n\nThese are systemic problems identified by reading specific files at line level. They represent the gap between \"it works\" and \"it's correct.\"\n\n### The `graph: Any` gap — most consequential issue\n\nThe central graph domain object is passed as `Any` throughout the codebase:\n- `qc/store.py`: `persist_graph_scorecards(graph: Any, ...)`\n- `conditional_edge_quality.py`: `_node_context(graph: Any, ...)`\n- Dozens more\n\nEvery `getattr(graph, \"run_id\", \"\")` and `getattr(graph, \"nodes\", {})` in production code is untyped dynamic dispatch — the type checker provides zero guarantees over the graph's structure. A single `Protocol`:\n\n```python\nclass GraphLike(Protocol):\n    run_id: str\n    nodes: Mapping[str, NodeLike]\n```\n\n...would eliminate dozens of `getattr` calls and let the checker catch field name typos and shape mismatches.\n\n### `dict[str, Any]` on domain dataclass fields\n\nFields that have known shapes typed as `Any`:\n- `qc/store.py`: `input_json: dict[str, Any]`, `output_json: dict[str, Any]`, `axes: dict[str, Any]`, `index_json: dict[str, Any]`\n- `contracts/questions.py`: `data: dict[str, Any]` on `QuestionRead` DTO\n- `tiers.py`: `dict[str, Any]` cache return types\n\n`Any` propagates — every downstream caller is forced into `Any`-land and the type checker stops helping. These should be `TypedDict` definitions or Pydantic models for the known shapes.\n\n### Import-time filesystem side effects in Fermi files\n\n```python\n# fermi_target.py, fermi_driver.py, fermi_indicator.py\nSYSTEM_PROMPT = _load_system_prompt()  # filesystem read at import time\n```\n\nImporting these modules unconditionally hits the filesystem. Tests that import them silently depend on the prompt store. An import failure crashes the process. Fix: lazy-load with `@lru_cache` inside `build_user_prompt`, or inject the prompt from the shell layer.\n\n### `_ensure_database()` called inside read functions\n\nEvery read in `qc/store.py` (e.g. `load_question_qc`, `list_question_scorecards`) calls `_ensure_database()` which runs `create_all()` — a DDL write — as a side effect. A read operation should never trigger schema creation. Schema init belongs at application startup.\n\n### `assert` as a runtime guard\n\n`assert stored is not None` in production code (`qc/store.py:152`). `assert` is disabled with `-O`. This is not a guard — it is documentation that vanishes in optimised builds. Should be `raise RuntimeError(...)` or a typed result type.\n\n### `except Exception` with swallowed errors\n\n`conditional_edge_quality.py`: a bare `except Exception` converts a real error into a dict entry, marked `# pragma: no cover — defensive guard`. An untested exception branch that swallows errors is a silent failure path. If `combine_odds` can fail, its failure modes should be typed and handled explicitly.\n\n### Private symbol imports across modules\n\n```python\nfrom packages.storage.graph_store import _edges_payload, _write_json\n```\n\nUnderscore-prefixed symbols are module-private by convention. Importing them couples this file to internal implementation of `graph_store`. Make them public or extract to shared utilities.\n\n### Truthiness bug in Fermi files\n\n```python\nhorizon_text = f\"{horizon_days} days\" if horizon_days else (horizon_bucket or \"unknown\")\n```\n\nIf `horizon_days = 0`, this evaluates as falsy and falls through to `horizon_bucket or \"unknown\"`, producing `\"unknown\"` instead of `\"0 days\"`. Should be `if horizon_days is not None`.\n\n### `str` where `Literal`/`StrEnum` belongs — contracts\n\nThroughout `contracts/questions.py`:\n- `target_type: str  # \"binary\" | \"categorical\" | \"numeric_ordinal\" | \"time_ordinal\"` — comment is not a type\n- `classification: str = \"binary\"` — appears on 5+ models\n- `relevance: str = \"direct\"` — appears on 2+ models\n\nAll should be `StrEnum` or `Literal`. A comment listing valid values means every consumer is on the honour system with no checker enforcement.\n\n### `\"\"` as a `None` sentinel\n\n`model_used: str = \"\"` on multiple contract models. An empty string to mean \"not set\" cannot be distinguished from \"set to empty\" — every downstream check uses `if model_used:` which conflates absence with emptiness. Use `str | None = None`.\n\n### Redundant `as_dict()` on frozen dataclasses\n\nSeveral dataclasses define manual `as_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]` methods. `dataclasses.asdict(instance)` does this for free and stays correct when fields are added. Manual methods go stale silently. At minimum the return type should not be `dict[str, Any]` — use a `TypedDict`.\n\n### Redundant type aliases without `TypeAlias`\n\nIn `tiers.py`:\n```python\nLLMProfileSelection = LLMModelSetSelection  # backward compat alias\n```\n\nWithout `TypeAlias` annotation (Python 3.10+) or `type` statement (3.12+), the type checker cannot distinguish this from a value assignment. Should be:\n```python\nLLMProfileSelection: TypeAlias = LLMModelSetSelection  # deprecated alias\n```\n\n### Tier enum aliases at module level\n\n```python\nTIER_SMALL = LLMTier.SMALL\nTIER_MEDIUM = LLMTier.MEDIUM\nTIER_LARGE = LLMTier.LARGE\n```\n\nThese are module-level names for enum members. If they exist for backward compat, document that. If callers can just import `LLMTier.SMALL`, these are pure noise.\n\n### `postprocess(result: Any)` on typed tools\n\n`ConditionalEdgeQualityTool.postprocess` takes `result: Any` and then duck-type-checks it:\n```python\nif hasattr(result, \"model_dump\"):\n    payload = result.model_dump(mode=\"json\")\nelif isinstance(result, dict):\n    payload = dict(result)\nelse:\n    payload = {}  # silent empty dict on unknown type\n```\n\nThe third branch silently returns an empty dict if `result` is an unexpected type. This should raise. The `Any` input type is also wrong — the `response_model` class attribute specifies the expected type; `postprocess` should declare `result: _ConditionalEdgeQualityOutput`.\n\n---\n\n## Approach\n\nGrep the full codebase for each pattern, file tickets or fix in place depending on scope. Reference `lookout.md` as the authoritative list.\n\nHighest-priority items before any investor technical review:\n\n1. Strip the `Role: / Where It Is Used:` boilerplate from module docstrings (cosmetic but an immediate tell)\n2. Clean the storage mapper defensive wrapping (most visible per-file code smell)\n3. Break up `_run_single_step_impl()` and `run_research_driver_for_question()` — 1,000+ line functions are the clearest signal of unreviewed generated code\n4. Extract a config dataclass from the 45-parameter `run_research_driver_over_questions()` signature\n5. Define a `GraphLike` Protocol — eliminates the `graph: Any` cascade across the codebase\n6. Fix the Fermi import-time side effect and the `horizon_days` truthiness bug\n7. Replace `str` with `StrEnum`/`Literal` on contract fields that have known valid values\n8. Refactor `retrieval_packs.py` copy-pasted classes into a proper hierarchy\n9. Extract a base class for the three `fermi_*.py` files\n10. Split `packages/storage/__init__.py` barrel alongside the `sql_repositories.py` split\n\n## Tooling\n\n**`vulture`** — static dead code detector. Run with:\n\n```\nuvx vulture packages/ core/ services/ --min-confidence 80\n```\n\nNote: running against `.` hits a recursion error; scope to source dirs only.\n\n**`ruff --select F401`** — catches unused imports:\n\n```\nuv run ruff check . --select F401\n```\n\n**`mypy --strict`** — would surface most of the `Any` / `Optional` / missing return type issues above. Not currently in CI.\n\n**Limitations of automated tools:**\n\n- Neither vulture nor ruff catches code that is reachable but never actually invoked in practice.\n- `mypy --strict` will produce many findings on first run; triage by file rather than fixing all at once.\n- Manual grep for callers remains the authoritative check for any function flagged as suspicious."
}

They represent the gap between \"it works\" and \"it's correct.\"\n\n### The `graph: Any` gap — most consequential issue\n\nThe central graph domain object is passed as `Any` throughout the codebase:\n\n* `qc/store.py`: `persist_graph_scorecards(graph: Any, ...)`\n* `conditional_edge_quality.py`: `_node_context(graph: Any, ...)`\n* Dozens more\n\nEvery `getattr(graph, \"run_id\", \"\")` and `getattr(graph, \"nodes\", {})` in production code is untyped dynamic dispatch — the type checker provides zero guarantees over the graph's structure. A single `Protocol`:\n\n```python\nclass GraphLike(Protocol):\n    run_id: str\n    nodes: Mapping[str, NodeLike]\n```\n\n...would eliminate dozens of `getattr` calls and let the checker catch field name typos and shape mismatches.\n\n### `dict[str, Any]` on domain dataclass fields\n\nFields that have known shapes typed as `Any`:\n\n* `qc/store.py`: `input_json: dict[str, Any]`, `output_json: dict[str, Any]`, `axes: dict[str, Any]`, `index_json: dict[str, Any]`\n* `contracts/questions.py`: `data: dict[str, Any]` on `QuestionRead` DTO\n* `tiers.py`: `dict[str, Any]` cache return types\n\n`Any` propagates — every downstream caller is forced into `Any`-land and the type checker stops helping. These should be `TypedDict` definitions or Pydantic models for the known shapes.\n\n### Import-time filesystem side effects in Fermi files\n\n```python\n# fermi_target.py, fermi_driver.py, fermi_indicator.py\nSYSTEM_PROMPT = _load_system_prompt()  # filesystem read at import time\n```\n\nImporting these modules unconditionally hits the filesystem. Tests that import them silently depend on the prompt store. An import failure crashes the process. Fix: lazy-load with `@lru_cache` inside `build_user_prompt`, or inject the prompt from the shell layer.\n\n### `_ensure_database()` called inside read functions\n\nEvery read in `qc/store.py` (e.g. `load_question_qc`, `list_question_scorecards`) calls `_ensure_database()` which runs `create_all()` — a DDL write — as a side effect. A read operation should never trigger schema creation. Schema init belongs at application startup.\n\n### `assert` as a runtime guard\n\n`assert stored is not None` in production code (`qc/store.py:152`). `assert` is disabled with `-O`. This is not a guard — it is documentation that vanishes in optimised builds. Should be `raise RuntimeError(...)` or a typed result type.\n\n### `except Exception` with swallowed errors\n\n`conditional_edge_quality.py`: a bare `except Exception` converts a real error into a dict entry, marked `# pragma: no cover — defensive guard`. An untested exception branch that swallows errors is a silent failure path. If `combine_odds` can fail, its failure modes should be typed and handled explicitly.\n\n### Private symbol imports across modules\n\n```python\nfrom packages.storage.graph_store import _edges_payload, _write_json\n```\n\nUnderscore-prefixed symbols are module-private by convention. Importing them couples this file to internal implementation of `graph_store`. Make them public or extract to shared utilities.\n\n### Truthiness bug in Fermi files\n\n```python\nhorizon_text = f\"{horizon_days} days\" if horizon_days else (horizon_bucket or \"unknown\")\n```\n\nIf `horizon_days = 0`, this evaluates as falsy and falls through to `horizon_bucket or \"unknown\"`, producing `\"unknown\"` instead of `\"0 days\"`. Should be `if horizon_days is not None`.\n\n### `str` where `Literal`/`StrEnum` belongs — contracts\n\nThroughout `contracts/questions.py`:\n\n* `target_type: str  # \"binary\" | \"categorical\" | \"numeric_ordinal\" | \"time_ordinal\"` — comment is not a type\n* `classification: str = \"binary\"` — appears on 5+ models\n* `relevance: str = \"direct\"` — appears on 2+ models\n\nAll should be `StrEnum` or `Literal`. A comment listing valid values means every consumer is on the honour system with no checker enforcement.\n\n### `\"\"` as a `None` sentinel\n\n`model_used: str = \"\"` on multiple contract models. An empty string to mean \"not set\" cannot be distinguished from \"set to empty\" — every downstream check uses `if model_used:` which conflates absence with emptiness. Use `str | None = None`.\n\n### Redundant `as_dict()` on frozen dataclasses\n\nSeveral dataclasses define manual `as_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]` methods. `dataclasses.asdict(instance)` does this for free and stays correct when fields are added. Manual methods go stale silently. At minimum the return type should not be `dict[str, Any]` — use a `TypedDict`.\n\n### Redundant type aliases without `TypeAlias`\n\nIn `tiers.py`:\n\n```python\nLLMProfileSelection = LLMModelSetSelection  # backward compat alias\n```\n\nWithout `TypeAlias` annotation (Python 3.10+) or `type` statement (3.12+), the type checker cannot distinguish this from a value assignment. Should be:\n\n```python\nLLMProfileSelection: TypeAlias = LLMModelSetSelection  # deprecated alias\n```\n\n### Tier enum aliases at module level\n\n```python\nTIER_SMALL = LLMTier.SMALL\nTIER_MEDIUM = LLMTier.MEDIUM\nTIER_LARGE = LLMTier.LARGE\n```\n\nThese are module-level names for enum members. If they exist for backward compat, document that. If callers can just import `LLMTier.SMALL`, these are pure noise.\n\n### `postprocess(result: Any)` on typed tools\n\n`ConditionalEdgeQualityTool.postprocess` takes `result: Any` and then duck-type-checks it:\n\n```python\nif hasattr(result, \"model_dump\"):\n    payload = result.model_dump(mode=\"json\")\nelif isinstance(result, dict):\n    payload = dict(result)\nelse:\n    payload = {}  # silent empty dict on unknown type\n```\n\nThe third branch silently returns an empty dict if `result` is an unexpected type. This should raise. The `Any` input type is also wrong — the `response_model` class attribute specifies the expected type; `postprocess` should declare `result: _ConditionalEdgeQualityOutput`.\n\n---\n\n## Approach\n\nGrep the full codebase for each pattern, file tickets or fix in place depending on scope. Reference `lookout.md` as the authoritative list.\n\nHighest-priority items before any investor technical review:\n\n 1. Strip the `Role: / Where It Is Used:` boilerplate from module docstrings (cosmetic but an immediate tell)\n 2. Clean the storage mapper defensive wrapping (most visible per-file code smell)\n 3. Break up `_run_single_step_impl()` and `run_research_driver_for_question()` — 1,000+ line functions are the clearest signal of unreviewed generated code\n 4. Extract a config dataclass from the 45-parameter `run_research_driver_over_questions()` signature\n 5. Define a `GraphLike` Protocol — eliminates the `graph: Any` cascade across the codebase\n 6. Fix the Fermi import-time side effect and the `horizon_days` truthiness bug\n 7. Replace `str` with `StrEnum`/`Literal` on contract fields that have known valid values\n 8. Refactor `retrieval_packs.py` copy-pasted classes into a proper hierarchy\n 9. Extract a base class for the three `fermi_*.py` files\n10. Split `packages/storage/__init__.py` barrel alongside the `sql_repositories.py` split\n\n## Tooling\n\n`vulture` — static dead code detector. Run with:\n\n```\nuvx vulture packages/ core/ services/ --min-confidence 80\n```\n\nNote: running against `.` hits a recursion error; scope to source dirs only.\n\n`ruff --select F401` — catches unused imports:\n\n```\nuv run ruff check . --select F401\n```\n\n`mypy --strict` — would surface most of the `Any` / `Optional` / missing return type issues above. Not currently in CI.\n\n**Limitations of automated tools:**\n\n* Neither vulture nor ruff catches code that is reachable but never actually invoked in practice.\n* `mypy --strict` will produce many findings on first run; triage by file rather than fixing all at once.\n* Manual grep for callers remains the authoritative check for any function flagged as suspicious.","priority":{"value":3,"name":"Medium"},"url":"https://linear.app/spire-elijah/issue/SPI-252/code-quality-sweep-remove-dead-code-defensive-wrapping-and-env-var","gitBranchName":"sumeet/spi-252-code-quality-sweep-remove-dead-code-defensive-wrapping-and","createdAt":"2026-05-12T14:55:23.345Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-12T18:06:48.462Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Backlog","statusType":"backlog","labels":[],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"sumeet@sumeetsaini.com","createdById":"c7a8f74f-a7ba-41e8-b771-85fc97b42216","assignee":"sumeet@sumeetsaini.com","assigneeId":"c7a8f74f-a7ba-41e8-b771-85fc97b42216","project":"Elijah Pipeline Development","projectId":"cf9fb1a1-0344-45e5-933a-a689a00d3858","team":"Spire_Elijah","teamId":"6fc98d18-8a80-4c49-8c42-a6e2a4d3d722"}

`int(time.time())` used as an ID component in `run_id` — not monotonic, collision-prone if two tools start the same second. Now I have ample evidence. Let me compose the report.

# Audit findings: Database, transaction, and async/sync correctness

Repo root: `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah`. All line numbers verified against current `port/spi-228-graph-gate-repair` worktree.

## 1. Database / SQLAlchemy patterns

**1. Mass N+1 in `_node_row_to_record` / `_edge_row_to_record` loops.** `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py:851, 856, 861, 873, 887, 892, 897, 912, 927, 1221, 1222` — every list comprehension `[_node_row_to_record(row, session=session) for row in rows]` triggers `_load_node_type_detail` (line 504-523) which issues one `session.get(TargetRow|DriverRow|...)` per row. For N nodes you get 1 + N queries; for edges (lines 526-544 plus 636-672) you get 1 + 2N because `_load_edge_type_detail` *also* fetches the parent `EdgeRow` at line 660 inside the same per-row helper. No `selectinload` / `joinedload` *anywhere* in the codebase (verified via grep).

**2. N+1 inside graph fan-out.** `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py:470-471` (`_graph_row_to_record`) runs two `select(NodeRow.node_id).where(...)` / `select(EdgeRow.edge_id).where(...)` per graph, called from the `load_graphs` list comprehension at line 851 — pure 1 + 2*G query pattern.

**3. Hand-rolled N+1 in `save_graphs`.** `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py:1236-1240` — `for nid in record.node_ids: ... session.get(NodeRow, nid)` to derive `first_node_qid`. Worst case: one query per node id per graph being saved.

**4. Whole-table scan to build lookup.** `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py:1260` and `:1274` — `session.scalars(select(NodeRow)).all()` (and same for `EdgeRow`) just to construct an in-memory `{node_id: graph_id}` dict. Loads every row in the table before a delete-all-then-rewrite.

**5. Delete-all-then-rewrite as the save model.** `save_nodes`, `save_edges`, and `save_graph_store_bundle` (`sql_repositories.py:1261-1266, 1273-1278, 1384-1416`) issue `delete(...)` against every node, edge, type table, and graph, then re-add. This pattern guarantees catastrophic transaction sizes, defeats incremental change tracking, and on Postgres serializes any concurrent reads against the table-level lock waits.

**6. `create_all()` invoked from repository constructors.** `sql_repositories.py:824, 1585, 1617, 1685` (and `packages/domain/qc/store.py:69`, `packages/domain/qc/gating_store.py:53`, `packages/domain/qc/research_defensibility_store.py:52`). DDL runs every time a repository is *instantiated*. On SQLite it's a `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` storm; on Postgres it's a no-op only because `database.py:2436-2438` short-circuits. Still: schema bootstrap inside the read-path code is a smell and a foot-gun.

**7. `create_all` *plus* Alembic.** `packages/storage/database.py:2435-2440` calls `Base.metadata.create_all(...)` for SQLite alongside an Alembic-managed Postgres schema (`database.py:2511-2516`). Schema-drift risk is high: SQLite tables are created by ORM declarations whose evolution is not necessarily mirrored by Alembic migrations.

**8. Raw DDL outside Alembic.** `packages/storage/database.py:2469-2471` runs `CREATE SEQUENCE IF NOT EXISTS node_id_seq` / `edge_id_seq` via `engine.begin()`. This is application code creating Postgres sequences outside migrations — exactly the schema-drift pattern Alembic is meant to prevent.

**9. Process-global `_DATABASE_CACHE`.** `packages/storage/database.py:2489-2502` — engines are cached by URL with no eviction, no `dispose()` hook, and no thread/process-fork awareness. If `resolve_storage_database` is called pre-fork (e.g. uvicorn workers), child processes will share the parent engine's pooled file descriptors / sockets — classic SQLAlchemy fork-safety bug.

**10. Connection pool defaults baked in, no recycle.** `packages/storage/database.py:2428-2432` hardcodes `pool_size=10`, `max_overflow=20`, `pool_pre_ping=True`, but no `pool_recycle` — long-lived idle connections will hit MySQL/Postgres idle-disconnect timeouts and rely on `pool_pre_ping` to recover (extra round-trip per checkout).

**11. SQLite `check_same_thread=False` without explicit serialization.** `database.py:2424-2425`. The pool used will be `SingletonThreadPool` by default, but with `check_same_thread=False` plus `ThreadPoolExecutor` calls (`services/worker/research_driver_batch_pipeline.py:631`) you can get concurrent writes against a single SQLite connection. No `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` discipline.

**12. ORM objects leak past `session_scope`.** `services/api/routes/graphs.py:181-206` — `with db.session_scope() as session: run = session.get(RunRow, run_id) ... return RunDetailResponse(run=PipelineRunRead(...))`. The Pydantic response is built inside the `with`, but `RunStepRead` list comp at line 207 reads `s.step_name`, `s.status`, etc. of every step row, which is fine only because `expire_on_commit=False`. Any access of a relationship not eagerly loaded after commit would raise `DetachedInstanceError`. This is a latent bomb.

**13. Reads and writes commingled in `session_scope`.** Every method in `SqlGraphStoreRepository` opens a write-capable `session_scope` (which always commits at exit, `database.py:2477-2483`) — even pure read methods like `load_nodes`, `load_edges`, etc. Commits on read paths is wasteful and produces spurious WAL writes / fsync on SQLite.

**14. Sessions silently committing.** `database.py:2478` — `session_scope` *always* commits on a clean exit. There is no read-only counterpart. Combined with the leaky ORM objects above, callers cannot opt out of a write transaction.

**15. `_ensure_question_row_from_file` loops with per-row `session.get` + late `session.flush()`.** `sql_repositories.py:251-254`. Re-fetches each row's primary key one at a time, then flushes once. For large imports this is N round trips before a single network flush.

**16. Hardcoded "first match" heuristic over a query.** `sql_repositories.py:1408-1411` (`first_node_qid = next(... for n in nodes if n.node_id in (record.node_ids or [])...)`) — does a Python-side O(N*M) iteration when a single `select(NodeRow.question_id).where(NodeRow.node_id.in_(record.node_ids)).limit(1)` would suffice.

**17. `relationship_description` column overloaded as JSON-blob backchannel.** `sql_repositories.py:660-671, 736-751` — `EdgeRow.relationship_description` is treated as `Text` in the schema but used as a JSON serializer for "extra" edge fields not modeled in `ConditionalEdgeRow` / `ReferenceEdgeRow`. This is the textbook "stuff a dict into a string column" smell; queries against these fields are impossible.

## 2. Async/sync mixing

**18. Sync DB / file I/O inside `async def` upload handlers.** `services/api/routes/documents.py:388-465` (`upload_question_documents`) — invokes `storage_adapters.graph_store_repository.load_graphs/nodes/edges/questions` (synchronous SQLAlchemy + JSON file reads, lines 395-398), `write_upload_file` (sync FS, lines 458, 465), and `sha256_bytes` (CPU). Same pattern at `services/api/routes/documents.py:740-807` (`upload_historic_series`, lines 747, 807). Every byte of every uploaded document blocks the event loop. FastAPI would not offload because the function is `async def`.

**19. Sync DB inside FastAPI lifespan.** `services/api/app.py:62-71` — `lifespan` calls `check_litellm_models()` which runs a *synchronous* `httpx.Client` request (`services/api/litellm_check.py:37-39`). Lifespan is async; blocking httpx I/O here blocks startup.

**20. `time` imported inside an async middleware hot path.** `services/api/app.py:122-130` — `async def request_tracing` does `import time` and `import uuid` on every single request. Cheap, but unnecessary churn; also `time.perf_counter()` for measurement is fine, but the import-inside-handler pattern is a code smell.

**21. No async route ever offloads to `run_in_executor`.** Search confirmed zero occurrences of `run_in_executor`, `asyncio.to_thread`, or `anyio.to_thread`. Either every `async def` route is wrong (sync inside async — see #18) or every route should have been `def` (FastAPI threadpools sync routes automatically). The codebase has chosen *neither* discipline.

**22. `threading.Thread` daemon + `ThreadPoolExecutor` lifecycle.** `services/worker/queue_runner.py:61, 174` — daemon heartbeat thread plus a 1-worker `ThreadPoolExecutor` wrapping a long-running blocking call so that a `future.result(timeout=...)` can fire. The `future.cancel()` at line 190 *will not interrupt the running task* (Python's cancel only works on not-yet-started futures); the worker leaks until the underlying call completes.

## 3. Resource / context manager discipline

**23. `requests.Session` never closed.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/http_client.py:36-37` — `ExternalHttpClient._session = requests.Session()` is created in `__init__` with no `__enter__`/`__exit__`/`close()`/`__del__`. Every instantiated client leaks its connection pool.

**24. `tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(delete=False)` with cleanup in `finally` that can NameError.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/pdf_download.py:78-90` — `tmp_path` is assigned inside the `with` block but used in `finally`. If `NamedTemporaryFile()` itself raises (disk full, no /tmp), `tmp_path` is unbound and the `finally` raises `NameError`, swallowed by the inner `try/except Exception`. Cleanup never happens.

**25. `subprocess.Popen` without `with`.** `tools/cli/commands.py:710, 779` — bare `Popen()`s collected into lists. Cleanup depends on `_stop_all` running on every code path, including signal handlers. No `try/finally` covers the entire lifetime in the spawning function.

## 4. Time / datetime correctness

**26. Deprecated naive `datetime.utcnow()`.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/insight_agent/reduce.py:90` — `datetime.utcnow().strftime(...)`. Returns naive UTC; deprecated in Python 3.12+.

**27. Naive `datetime.now()` (local time) used in QC store.** `packages/domain/qc/gating_store.py:61, 341` — `datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat()`. This snaps to *local* timezone, not UTC, and embeds the local offset into a string used as `generated_at`. Inconsistent with the rest of the repo, which uses `datetime.now(UTC)`.

**28. Module-import-time year freeze.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/tiered_retrieval.py:29` — `_CURRENT_YEAR = datetime.now().year` is evaluated *at import* using a naive `now()`. Won't roll over across New Year while the process is running.

**29. `time.time()` used as a logical sequence component.** `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py:1490` — `run_id=f"{question_id}-{int(time.time())}"`. Not monotonic (wall clock can go backwards across NTP corrections); collides if two runs start within the same second.

**30. `int(time.time())` as a filename salt.** `services/worker/shared_tools/web_tools.py:574` — `f"{page_id}_{int(time.time())}.png"`. Same issue, plus collisions across replicas.

**31. Hand-rolled `+00:00` -> `Z` replace, scattered.** Same string fix repeated ten+ times: `services/api/job_service.py:61`, `services/api/routes/{question_sets,graphs,question_decomposition,driver_promotion}.py`, `packages/storage/sql_repositories.py:196`, `packages/domain/baselines/apply.py:97`. Begs to be one helper.

## 5. Magic strings and numbers

**32. Hardcoded thread-pool sizes.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/retrieval_packs.py:2195` (`max_workers=4`), `core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py:366` (`max_workers=3`), `services/worker/research_driver_question_pipeline.py:1733, 2500` (`max_workers=2`), `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/agent.py:850, 1111` (`max_workers=4`). No central knob; tuning concurrency requires grepping.

**33. Hardcoded retry/backoff/timeout literals.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/http_client.py:44-46` (`max_attempts=4`, `base_backoff_seconds=1.5`, `timeout_seconds=15.0`) and `pdf_download.py:114-145` re-implements the same retry loop with the same constants. No shared retry policy.

**34. Magic status sets buried in dict literals.** `sql_repositories.py:960-968` `_STATUS_MAP` mixes `"running"`, `"completed"`, `"ok"`, `"partial"`, `"succeeded"`, `"failed"`, `"cancelled"` as plain lowercase strings rather than an enum. Same enums *do* exist in `database.py:169-174` (`RunStatus`) but are not used here.

**35. Hardcoded HTTP status code sets.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/http_client.py:29` defines `_RETRYABLE_STATUS_CODES = frozenset({429, 500, 502, 503, 504})` while `pdf_download.py:129` inlines the same tuple `(429, 500, 502, 503, 504)` literally — two sources of truth.

**36. `SystemExit(1)` raised from a library helper.** `services/api/litellm_check.py:47, 58, 68` — `raise SystemExit(1) from exc`. Called from FastAPI `lifespan`; this kills the worker process from inside an imported function. Hardcoded exit code, no graceful surface to the app's error machinery.

**37. UA string hardcoded.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/pdf_download.py:111` — `"User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; ElijahBot/1.0)"`. No central HTTP identity / version source.

Confirmed: worker entry uses `print()` for status output, no `configure_logging()` is called for worker. Logger calls happen in modules but never route to JSON sink because root logger is never configured by worker entry.

I have enough to compile a thorough audit. Let me finalize.

---

## Brutal audit findings

### 1. Logging discipline

**F1. Worker process never calls `configure_logging` — JSON logging is dead in production.** `core/logging.py:77` defines a JSON formatter and structured ContextVars (`trace_id`, `run_id`, `question_id`, `step_id`). Only `services/api/app.py:44` calls it, and it passes `json_output=False`. The worker entry path `tools/cli/commands.py:489` constructs `QueueWorkerRunner` without ever configuring logging — so every `logger.info(...)` from worker code falls back to Python's default `WARNING` root handler (lastResort), losing INFO logs entirely and never emitting JSON. The whole structured-log setup is theatre.

**F2. Three competing logging setups.** `core/logging.py:configure_logging` (JSON), `core/utils.py:92 _setup_logging` (basicConfig + file handler), and `core/research/tool_cli.py:187 logging.basicConfig` all live in the codebase and race. `core/utils.py:_setup_logging` is called by both `main.py:50` and `tools/cli/main.py:50`, immediately stomping any `configure_logging`-installed handlers with `basicConfig`. If a CLI entrypoint imports the API factory, you get whichever ran last.

**F3. `_CONFIGURED` global makes the logger uninitialisable in tests.** `core/logging.py:74 _CONFIGURED = False` then `configure_logging` early-returns if it was called before. Tests that need different log levels per case cannot re-init. Should be idempotent via state comparison or use `dictConfig`.

**F4. ContextVars are set-only, never reset.** `core/logging.py:24 set_trace_id`, `set_run_context` only call `.set()` — no `Token` returned, no `reset()`. In an async/threadpool worker the `run_id` from a prior job leaks into the next one. Should return `Token` or be a context-manager.

**F5. No structured-extras discipline.** `core/logging.py:63` whitelists `user_id, document_key, driver_id, node_id, job_id` for JSON output, but 287 logger calls in `services/` and 34 in `core/` overwhelmingly use positional `%s` formatting with no `extra={}`. The only modules using `extra=` are `core/utils.py:52`, `core/research_driver/document_review.py:676`, and `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:484`. The structured-log promise is unrealised for ~95% of call sites.

**F6. `litellm`/`httpx` muted twice in different places with different levels.** `core/logging.py:113` sets them WARNING, `core/utils.py:106` also sets them WARNING. Whichever loads later wins; the duplication is a latent bug.

**F7. `print()` used for operational status in worker boot.** `tools/cli/commands.py:488,493,498,500` use `print(f"Database: ...")`, `print(f"Queue worker processed ...")`. These should be logs so they end up in the JSON stream. `tools/cli/display.py` is the worst offender (~30+ `print()` statements for what is structurally pipeline-progress logging).

**F8. `logger.exception` vs `logger.error(..., exc_info=True)` is genuinely inconsistent.** 8 uses of `.exception()` vs 64 instances of `exc_info=`. The two forms have identical effect; the codebase oscillates. e.g. `services/api/routes/iw_trigger.py:111` uses `.exception()` while `core/research/historical_data.py:187,197,567` uses `exc_info=True`.

**F9. `logger.warning` swallows then re-raises pattern is inconsistent.** `core/pipelines/question_set_evaluation.py:166,331,872` logs warnings then falls back to a heuristic with no error code/traceback attached — debugging a "LLM edge assessment failed for X: <exc>" requires the full stack but only the str(exc) is captured. Should be `logger.warning("...", exc_info=True)`.

### 2. Error message quality

**F10. `RuntimeError`/`ValueError` sprawl in storage.** 207 untyped `raise RuntimeError`/`raise ValueError` in `packages/`, `core/`, `services/`. Storage layer is the worst: `packages/storage/document_store.py:108,120,133,140,249,280,282,285`, `packages/storage/fs2_state_store.py:136-444` (~25 raises), `packages/storage/prompt_trace_store.py:35-117`. These should all be a typed `StorageValidationError` so callers can `except` precisely.

**F11. `DomainError` hierarchy is good but only used in `services/api/`.** `core/errors.py:16` defines `DomainError/NotFoundError/ConflictError/ValidationError/PipelineError`, but only imported by `services/api/{graph,question,document}_service.py` and `services/api/app.py`. Worker code and core pipelines raise raw `ValueError`. The error taxonomy is not actually a contract.

**F12. "Unreachable" RuntimeErrors that aren't asserts.** `packages/inference/llm/runtime.py:614 raise RuntimeError("Unreachable: _call_with_model_fallback")`, also `:675`, `:717`. These should be `assert False, "..."` or a custom `InternalLogicError` — `RuntimeError` here loses semantic information at the catch site.

**F13. Inconsistent message register.** Good: `packages/storage/database.py:2451-2463` (`StorageSchemaNotReady`) explicitly tells the operator the command to run (`uv run python -m tools.cli.main storage-init`). Bad: `core/gjopen/scraper.py:488 raise ValueError("Could not find question text on the page")` — no URL, no selector tried, no class context. `services/worker/queue_runner.py:170 raise ValueError(f"Unsupported job type: {claimed.job_type}")` — no list of supported types.

**F14. `AttributeError` re-raised as documentation.** `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/research_agent/agent.py:212 raise AttributeError("...")` — `AttributeError` for a contract violation is a Python anti-pattern; the caller can't tell it apart from a real attribute miss.

**F15. `MarkItDownIngestError(RuntimeError)` is correctly typed and chains `from exc`.** `core/document_ingest.py:51,61,71,81` are exemplary: typed, chained, and the message includes path + actual exception type. The rest of the codebase should look like this.

**F16. `noqa: BLE001` and `except Exception` are routine.** 232 bare-`except Exception` blocks in `core/packages/services`. `core/gjopen/scraper.py:372-387` swallows 4 in a row with no logging. This makes debugging silent failures impossible.

### 3. Configuration management

**F17. Hand-rolled dotenv loader runs at import-time and silently `setdefault`s.** `core/settings.py:19 _load_dotenv_if_present` parses `.env` with `str.partition("=")`. No `pydantic.Settings`, no quoting, no multi-line, no `export` keyword, no `${VAR}` expansion. Crashes on `KEY=value=foo` because `value.strip("'\"")` will mangle legitimate values. Use `pydantic-settings`.

**F18. `ENV_VARS` registry is documentation only.** `core/settings.py:185-212` lists 22 env vars with descriptions but there's no startup validation that required vars are present, no type registry, no parser registry. `DATABASE_URL` is validated at `packages/storage/runtime_config.py:21` only when constructed, not at app boot — fail-late.

**F19. Configuration scattered across 4 surfaces.** (a) `core/settings.py` constants, (b) `config/*.toml` (run_profile, system_defaults, llm_tiers, llm_tasks), (c) `os.environ` reads in 35+ files outside `core/settings.py`, (d) per-module `_FLAG = env_text(...)` reads (e.g. `core/research_driver/conditional_edge_quality.py:39`, `packages/domain/baselines/llm_prior.py:62`, `packages/domain/baselines/fermi_llm.py:70`, `packages/domain/qc/llm_scorer.py:76` each read their own `ELIJAH_*_FLAG` env var directly).

**F20. Raw `os.environ.get` in hot paths.** `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:213,221,224,389` reads `LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL`/`LITELLM_API_KEY` four separate times per call. Should be resolved once at construction.

**F21. Feature flags live as ad-hoc env strings, no central registry.** `ELIJAH_LLM_PRIORS`, `_FERMI_BASELINE_FLAG`, `_FERMI_EDGE_FLAG`, `_FS11_LLM_FLAG`, `_SYSTEM_DEFAULTS_ENV` — every one of these is its own `os.environ.get(...).strip().lower() in ("1","true","yes","on")` cargo-cult. `core/settings.env_bool` exists but isn't used.

**F22. No distinction between secrets and config.** `ELIJAH_API_KEY`, `LITELLM_API_KEY`, `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY`, `*_API_KEY` are in the same `ENV_VARS` dict as `OBJECT_STORE_PREFIX` and `QUEUE_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS`. No SecretStr, no redaction policy. If anything logs `ApiConfig`, the api_key goes to disk in plaintext (it's a plain `dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)` field at `services/api/config.py:30`).

**F23. `_load_dotenv_if_present` uses `setdefault` so explicit env wins — but executes at *import time*.** Importing `core.settings` mutates `os.environ` globally as a side effect. Test isolation breaks.

**F24. `_DATABASE_CACHE` and `_DATABASE_STORAGE_CACHE` are mutable module-level singletons keyed on URL.** `packages/storage/database.py:2489` and `packages/storage/worker.py:57`. No eviction, no thread-safety primitives, leaks across test runs unless tests explicitly clear them.

### 4. Test quality

**F25. Generic conftest with one fixture, but `tests/support/` has 880 lines of helper code.** `tests/conftest.py` is only 41 lines with a single `graph_store_repo` fixture (good). But `tests/support/api_runtime.py` (324 lines) and `tests/support/regression_fixture.py` (555 lines) ship a parallel fixture/factory framework that test files import explicitly — this is helper-sprawl masquerading as cleanliness.

**F26. Mock-heavy tests with brittle `patch()` strings.** `tests/test_analyst_review.py:91-299` uses `MagicMock(spec=...)` 7+ times per file and patches `services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.analyst_review._single_source_search` by string — a refactor that moves the module breaks tests silently. Worse, `tests/test_pdf_download.py:21` patches `pdf_download._download_bytes` (a private symbol) which is exactly the test antipattern: testing the implementation, not the contract.

**F27. Mock-based "tests" that just verify the mock was called.** `tests/test_litellm_check.py:155 mock.get.assert_called_once()` followed by `:157 assert call_kwargs[1]["headers"]["Authorization"] == "Bearer sk-test-key"`. This proves the test setup works, not that the system works. Should hit a fake HTTP server (e.g. `pytest-httpserver`).

**F28. Zero `pytest-asyncio` usage.** `grep -c "@pytest.mark.asyncio" tests/*.py == 0` — yet the API is FastAPI (asyncio-native). Tests use `fastapi.testclient.TestClient` (sync) only. No tests exercise the async lifespan, cancellation, or concurrent request paths.

**F29. Hardcoded `SimpleNamespace` for domain stubs.** `tests/test_iw_trigger.py:34-90`, `tests/test_bn_inference.py:11-79` build domain graphs out of `SimpleNamespace`. This bypasses validation and means tests can construct states the production code would reject. Should use real `NodeRecord`/`EdgeRecord` constructors or proper factories.

**F30. `tests/test_indicator_graph_apply.py` is 1548 lines.** Largest test file; almost certainly a kitchen-sink integration test that no one will read. Test suites this large hide what's actually covered.

**F31. Parametrize used but sparsely.** 24 `@pytest.mark.parametrize` across 148 test files; `tests/test_relationship_judgment.py:64,70` is the only file that systematically parametrizes over a canonical enum (good). Most "similar tests" are copy-pasted (see `tests/test_pdf_download.py:9-110` with 5+ nearly-identical functions).

**F32. DB tests use real SQLite — good — but only round-trip.** `tests/test_storage_repositories.py:56,82` constructs a real `StorageRuntimeConfig` against SQLite and round-trips. Excellent. But no test exercises the PostgreSQL-only paths (`packages/storage/database.py:2467 _bootstrap_id_sequences`, ENUMs, FK cascades). PG-specific behaviour is untested.

**F33. Test naming inconsistency.** `tests/test_relationship_judgment.py:31 test_normalize_relationship_judgment_accepts_gradient_aliases` (great) vs `tests/test_api_service.py:48 test_question_crud_creates_canonical_starter_graph_updates_graph_and_allows_starter_delete` (single 893-line file with kitchen-sink test names). Naming convention drifts file-to-file.

### 5. Pydantic / dataclass / TypedDict consistency

**F34. 119 BaseModel, 133 @dataclass, 2 TypedDict — and the rule isn't visible.** Internal data carriers (`packages/storage/database.py` rows) are `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)`. LLM I/O (`core/research_driver/iw_verdict_tool.py:31`, `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:123`) is `BaseModel`. API contracts (`packages/contracts/question_sets.py:20`) are `BaseModel`. But CLI/run config (`core/run_config.py:11`) is `@dataclass`, even though the surface is user-facing TOML — exactly where Pydantic's validation would help most. There is no `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` documenting the choice.

**F35. `frozen=True` is inconsistent.** 113 of 133 dataclasses are frozen. The non-frozen ones include `core/pipelines/graph_persistence.py:41`, `core/research_driver/data_model.py:80,93`, `services/worker/research_driver_step_pipeline.py:130` — these mutate during pipeline execution which makes reasoning about state transitions impossible.

**F36. `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="ignore")` everywhere.** `research/question_domains.py:105,114`, `core/research_driver/iw_verdict_tool.py:32,99`, `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:123,134`. Silently dropping unknown fields from LLM output is a debugging nightmare — when the LLM adds a field, you discover it via a missing-data bug, not a validation error. Should be `extra="forbid"` for LLM outputs at minimum.

**F37. TypedDict used exactly twice.** `packages/inference/llm/types.py:25 class Message(TypedDict)`. Should either be a `BaseModel` (validated) or just `dict[str, Any]` (honest). TypedDict is the worst of both worlds: no validation, but Python users think they have one.

### 6. Dependency injection vs globals

**F38. `default_prompt_store` is `@cache`-decorated, returning a process-wide singleton.** `packages/inference/prompts/store.py:37 @cache _cached_prompt_store`. Every call site (`core/research_driver/question_decomposition_tool.py:47`, `core/research_driver/iw_verdict_tool.py:24,28`, `packages/domain/baselines/llm_prior.py:37`, etc — 12+ sites) calls `default_prompt_store().load("...")` inline. Tests have to monkeypatch the module attribute to inject a fake. This is hidden global state.

**F39. Second `default_prompt_store` shadow at `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/prompt_store.py:30`.** Wraps the canonical one with a different `render_mode="brace_safe"` and a different env var (`RESEARCH_DRIVER_PROMPTS_DIR`). Two functions with the same name and different semantics — anyone grepping for the call site finds either.

**F40. Storage builders also cache.** `packages/storage/worker.py:57 _DATABASE_STORAGE_CACHE`, `build_database_worker_storage_adapters` caches by URL string. Same singleton anti-pattern as the prompt store.

**F41. `_ACTIVE_MODEL_SET: ContextVar` is set via context manager `model_set_context` at `packages/inference/llm/tiers.py:42,86`.** Then resolution falls back to `os.environ.get(LLM_MODEL_SET_ENV)`. Two parallel injection channels (ContextVar + env) with precedence rules buried in `resolve_model_set_selection` at `:121-146`. Hard to predict at a call site which one wins.

**F42. `WorkerStorageAdapters` is the proper injection seam.** `packages/storage/worker.py:42` is a `@dataclass(frozen=True)` of repository fields with default factories — passed through `services/api/app.py:36 create_app(storage_adapters=...)`. This *is* good DI. The contrast with `default_prompt_store()` shows the codebase knows the right pattern but didn't apply it everywhere.

**F43. Test suite has to patch globals.** `tests/test_fermi_baseline_llm.py` (only file matching `monkeypatch.*default_`) is the only test that monkeypatches `default_prompt_store`. The other 11 call sites are effectively untestable in isolation without doing the same dance.

---

### Summary

The codebase has good intentions (structured JSON logger, DomainError hierarchy, env helper functions, `WorkerStorageAdapters` DI, frozen dataclasses) but they're applied unevenly. The structured logger is essentially dead in the worker. The DomainError hierarchy stops at the API boundary. Configuration is centralised in name only — 35+ files still read `os.environ` directly. Tests over-mock private functions and lean on `MagicMock(spec=...)` instead of real fakes. The fixes are mostly mechanical (audit env reads, apply `extra="forbid"`, wire `configure_logging` into the worker entry, replace `RuntimeError` with typed exceptions) but they aren't trivial because the patterns have already calcified across 100+ files.

Key files referenced: `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/core/logging.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/core/settings.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/core/errors.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/core/utils.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/packages/storage/database.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/packages/inference/llm/tiers.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/packages/inference/prompts/store.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/services/api/app.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/tools/cli/commands.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/tests/conftest.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/tests/test_analyst_review.py`, `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/tests/test_litellm_check.py`.
